## Local Gate Repairs

- `8bb9d6213`: moved the binary-literal extension coverage out of PA6 and into
  the PA33 host-compatibility preprocessor suite. The preprocessor now reports
  `__has_feature(__cxx_binary_literals__)` and
  `__has_extension(__cxx_binary_literals__)` as supported, matching the hosted
  binary integer literal and binary UDL support added for Boost.Asio. The old
  PA6 `0b01000010` fixture was replaced by a plain invalid-token balanced-scan
  reducer using `@`, preserving coverage for the `RecogTokenBuffer` invalid
  token EOF sentinel without assigning the hosted binary-literal extension to
  PA6. Validation: `make -C dev cppgm++ preproc recog -j12`; focused PA6
  reducer; full `make -C pa6 test CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1`; focused PA33
  binary-literal feature and tokenization checks; `ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa33'
  ORDERED=false TEST_REPORT_SUBTEST_JOBS=12 TEST_REPORT_ASSIGNMENT_JOBS=1
  CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1 make test-report-nobuild` passed `301/301`; direct
  Boost.Config `no_cxx14_binary_literals_fail.cpp` now fails quickly via the
  expected `#error`, while `no_cxx14_binary_literals_pass.cpp` compiles and
  runs with status `0`.
- `dcdd5d3e6`: restored the local lowir-compare gates before continuing Boost
  survey triage. Static hosted `typeid(T)` now uses an already requested local
  RTTI definition instead of an external ABI alias, fixing the PA34
  `std::function<int()>` link failure for `_ZTIPFivE` and the PA27 direct-text
  typeid drift. The temporary PA6 binary-literal reference refresh from this
  commit was corrected in `8bb9d6213`; the PA29 reference was refreshed for
  current `unwind=no` constructor metadata. Validation:
  focused `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa6 pa27 pa29 pa34'`
  report passed `229/229`; full `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 make
  test-report` passed `3070/3070`; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 make
  test-strict` passed.
- `e20ff04b4`: temporary local direct-LowIR gate refresh after the typed NTTP
  regression. Before the witness-output repair, the PA19
  `100-negative-nontype-substitution-comparison.t` reference was aligned to the
  then-current non-odr-used static-member output. Full
  `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ... make test-report-nobuild` passed
  `3076/3076`; the reference churn was superseded by `fbde86ce6`.
- `fbde86ce6`: repaired the PA19 direct-LowIR/witness drift from source-capture
  replay of unrequired static const integral members. Witness member-value
  dependencies are now tracked on `ValueBinding`; output suppresses only
  witness-only unrequired non-`value` static const integral members when there
  is no real member-output requirement, preserving canonical trait `value`
  globals and explicit specializations. The PA19
  `100-negative-nontype-substitution-comparison.t` reference stdout is empty
  again. Validation: witness/no-witness diffs are clean for the PA19 typed NTTP
  reducer, PA19 dependent conversion, PA19 explicit specialization, two PA21
  structured-value regressions, and two PA22 static-member regressions; focused
  `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa19' ... make
  test-report-nobuild` passed `105/105`; full strict direct-LowIR compare
  passed PA18, PA19, PA21, and PA22.
- PA26 direct-LowIR reference refresh after `069681317`: the braced
  constructor argument in
  `200-direct-init-single-braced-constructor-argument.t` now materializes
  directly in the target argument slot rather than through an extra temporary
  and `copyobj`. Focused PA26 check passes; focused
  `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa26' ... make
  test-report-nobuild` passes `99/99`; full direct-LowIR report passes
  `3077/3077`.
- `84dcf6840`: repaired the Boost.Bind `bind_mf2_test` typed member-function
  pointer overload path. Dependent class-template metadata now keeps structured
  semantic arguments authoritative when stale source text names a different
  template parameter, while only the narrow single-type-parameter display case
  updates printable dependent class names. PA28 LowIR refs were refreshed for
  the earlier const member-function-pointer mangling correction (`M...KF...`).
  Validation: focused Boost-shaped reducers pass; PA28 course regression
  passes; PA28 direct-LowIR report passes `50/50`; PA22 direct-LowIR report
  passes `742/742`; full strict direct-LowIR compare passes; full
  `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ... make test-report-nobuild` passes
  `3100/3100`; focused Boost `bind_mf2_test` passes; perf check against
  `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json`
  passes (instructions `-0.00%`, RSS `-1.06%`, footprint `+1.41%`).
- `c96b3232a`: repaired the Boost.Bind `bind_function_ap_test` runtime crash.
  `static_cast<T&&>(value)` now preserves the original object reference when
  the target is a direct same-object class reference, instead of considering a
  user-defined conversion function such as `std::auto_ptr<T>::operator
  auto_ptr<U>()`. Validation: no-STL and `auto_ptr` forwarding reducers run
  successfully; PA22 regression passes in normal and direct-LowIR modes; PA22
  reports pass `441/441`; full strict direct-LowIR compare passes; full
  `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ... make test-report-nobuild` passes
  `3101/3101`; focused Boost `bind_function_ap_test` passes; perf check
  against `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json`
  passes (instructions `+0.29%`, RSS `+1.97%`, footprint `+1.37%`).
- `6c5d1adc5`: repaired the Boost.Bind `bind_nested_rv_test` compile frontier.
  `boost::shared_ptr<int> p; p != 0` exposed that integral null pointer
  constants were not considered convertible to `nullptr_t`, so the viable
  `operator!=(shared_ptr<T> const&, nullptr_t)` overload was rejected. The
  conversion layer now accepts and retags that standard conversion, and overload
  tie-breaking compares source arguments for ordinary standard conversions
  while still comparing the post-conversion result for the second standard
  conversion in user-defined conversion sequences. Validation: PA12 conversion
  and ambiguity reducers pass; PA15 operator reducer passes in normal and
  direct-LowIR modes; PA12/PA15 report passes `267/267`; PA16 direct-LowIR
  report passes `139/139`; full strict direct-LowIR compare passes; full
  direct-LowIR report passes `3104/3104`; focused Boost `bind_nested_rv_test`
  compiles and advances to a separate link-time missing
  `boost::shared_ptr<int>` move constructor; perf check against
  `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json`
  passes (instructions `+0.35%`, RSS `+1.20%`, footprint `+1.36%`).
- `555f3ef01`: repaired the Boost.Bind `bind_nested_rv_test` link frontier.
  A by-value call through a function pointer,
  `function(static_cast<T&&>(value))`, needed the inline
  `boost::shared_ptr<int>` move constructor, but semantic output only collected
  hidden argument materialization support when the call target was a direct
  function binding. Indirect function-pointer calls now use the structured
  callee function type that CallSem already has, avoiding text reparsing while
  requiring the same hidden copy/move support as direct calls. The resulting
  PA18 LowIR reducer also exposed an output-on-use ordering issue where a move
  constructor could be emitted before the same complete-entry copy constructor;
  LowIR generation now builds a one-time copy-before-move prerequisite map for
  constructor definitions and uses an incremental emitted-symbol set rather
  than broad call-argument tracking. A broader runtime-reference pre-scan was
  rejected after perf regressed. Validation: no-STL reducer
  `/tmp/cppgm-boost-reducers-20260526/template_inline_move_ctor_emit.cpp`
  compiles, links, and runs with exit `0`; PA18 regression
  `100-template-function-pointer-rvalue-argument-emits-move.t` passes in
  normal and direct-LowIR modes; PA18 direct-LowIR report passes `167/167`;
  full `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ... make test-report-nobuild`
  passes `3105/3105`; full strict direct-LowIR compare passes; focused Boost
  `libs/bind/test//bind_nested_rv_test` builds, links, and runs under B2; perf
  check against
  `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json`
  passes (instructions `+0.18%`, RSS `+0.49%`, footprint `+1.36%`).
- `3e805a2e6`: repaired the Boost.Bind `apply_rv_test` /
  `apply_rv_test2` compile frontier. Forwarding function lvalues through
  Boost.Bind deduced argument types such as `F & (&)()`, and libc++'s
  `std::tuple<A&...>` constructor SFINAE asked
  `__is_constructible(F & (&)(), F & (&)())`. The structural builtin-trait
  evaluator treated every target whose unreferenced base was a function as an
  unconstructible function object type, even when the actual target was a
  reference. The evaluator now lets reference targets use the existing
  structured standard-conversion check, with no source reparsing. Validation:
  no-STL reducer
  `/tmp/cppgm-boost-reducers-20260526/constructible_function_ref_tuple_sfinae_nostl.cpp`
  and hosted libc++ tuple reducer
  `/tmp/cppgm-boost-reducers-20260526/tuple_function_reference_pack.cpp`
  compile; PA33 regression
  `771-builtin-constructible-function-ref-sfinae.t` passes; PA33 report passes
  `302/302`; full direct-LowIR report passes `3106/3106`; full strict
  direct-LowIR compare passes; focused Boost `apply_rv_test` and
  `apply_rv_test2` pass; full `JOBS=12 ./run-cppgm-b2.sh -a libs/bind/test`
  passes and updates 303 targets; perf check against
  `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json`
  passes (instructions `+0.26%`, RSS `+1.16%`, footprint `+1.35%`).
- `a6da73958`: repaired the first Boost.Bloom compile frontier. Qualified
  array type template arguments such as `std::uint32_t[4]` were recognized
  neither as direct qualified type-ids nor as dependent expressions by the
  template-argument fragment classifier, so the structured `type_id` syntax was
  dropped before explicit type-argument resolution and strict fallback auditing
  stopped in `filter_core::next_element`. The parser now treats `[]` after a
  non-template qualified type name as type syntax, preserving structured type
  resolution without adding a source-text reparse path. Validation: no-STL
  reducers
  `/tmp/cppgm-boost-reducers-20260526/qualified_array_type_template_arg_tiny.cpp`,
  `/tmp/cppgm-boost-reducers-20260526/nested_qualified_array_type_template_arg.cpp`,
  and `/tmp/cppgm-boost-reducers-20260526/bloom_array_type_arg_min.cpp` compile;
  PA18 regression
  `100-qualified-array-type-template-argument.t` passes; PA18 placement audit
  passes with `--fail-on-early`; PA18 report passes `168/168`; PA18
  direct-LowIR report passes `168/168`; full strict direct-LowIR compare
  passes; full direct-LowIR report passes `3107/3107`; focused Boost
  `libs/bloom/test//test_bulk_operations` and full `libs/bloom/test` advance to
  the next frontier, `invalid array braced-init-list` in
  `boost::mp11::detail::mp_for_each_impl`, with 9 compile targets still
  failing; perf check against
  `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json`
  passes (instructions `+0.26%`, RSS `+1.98%`, footprint `+1.40%`).
- 602485622: repaired the next Boost.Bloom compile frontier. Substitution
  while probing `used_value_size<subfilter>` already had the structured bound
  type `block<unsigned int[4], 4>`, but explicit type-argument resolution later
  saw only generated text `unsigned int [4]` and hit the hard fallback audit.
  The fix keeps the lookup narrow: when the fallback path scans local
  template-bound type names, it inspects the already-attached class-template
  specialization argument metadata and returns the matching `TypePtr`, without
  adding global tracking or reparsing the array type spelling. Validation:
  no-STL reducer
  `/tmp/cppgm-boost-reducers-20260526/used_value_size_array_text_fallback_nostl.cpp`
  compiles; PA22 regression
  `500-array-type-argument-sfinae-static-value.t` passes; PA22 placement audit
  passes with only existing backend-owner notes; PA22 report passes `442/442`
  in normal and direct-LowIR modes; full direct-LowIR report passes `3109/3109`
  at lower concurrency after representative high-parallel timeout failures pass
  singly; full strict direct-LowIR compare passes with a single strict worker;
  focused Boost `libs/bloom/test//test_bulk_operations` and full
  `libs/bloom/test` advance to `unknown function __builtin_prefetch`, with one
  additional Bloom target also exposing the separate `detail::umul128` lazy-body
  materialization frontier; perf check against
  `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json`
  passes (instructions `+0.35%`, RSS `+1.72%`, footprint `+1.38%`).
- `b2703f791`: repaired the next Boost.Bloom hosted-builtin compile frontier.
  `BOOST_BLOOM_PREFETCH` / `BOOST_BLOOM_PREFETCH_WRITE` expand to
  `__builtin_prefetch((const char*)p, ...)`, but the hosted builtin registry and
  `__has_builtin` table omitted `__builtin_prefetch`. The fix registers the
  one-, two-, and three-argument forms and lowers the call as an
  argument-evaluating LowIR no-op, matching the compile-time semantics Boost
  needs without adding any new tracking structure. Validation: stdin no-STL
  reducer advances from `unknown function __builtin_prefetch` to compiling;
  clang accepts the checked-in PA33 regression; PA33 regression
  `600-builtin-prefetch.t` passes; PA33 report passes `303/303`; PA33 placement
  audit marks the new test owner-reached at cluster 600, while the whole-PA
  audit still exits nonzero on pre-existing cluster-early PA33 tests; full
  direct-LowIR report passes `3110/3110`; full strict direct-LowIR compare
  passes with a single strict worker; focused Boost
  `libs/bloom/test//test_bulk_operations` advances to the unrelated
  `detail::umul128` lazy-body materialization frontier; full `libs/bloom/test`
  has 9 compile failures led by `detail::umul128`, with `test_insertion` also
  exposing `std::array<decltype(fac()),10>` instantiation; clean perf rerun
  against
  `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json`
  passes (instructions `+0.33%`, RSS `+1.70%`, footprint `+1.36%`).
- `d0ca97c2d`: repaired the next Boost.Bloom lazy-body materialization
  frontier. Both the eager and lazy AST parsers recognized GNU `__int128`, but
  not the hosted typedef spellings `__int128_t` and `__uint128_t`, as declaration
  specifiers or as cast/type-id starters. Boost.Bloom's `detail::umul128`
  declares `__uint128_t r = (__uint128_t)x * y;`, so lazy header-body
  materialization rejected the local declaration and cast before semantic
  analysis. The fix extends the existing GNU int128 parser predicate to those
  typedef spellings and consults it in type-specifier-seq and type-id lookahead;
  it adds no reparse path or new tracking structure. Validation: pre-fix PA33
  lazy-header reducer, direct primary-source `__uint128_t` local reducer, and
  Boost `mulx64.hpp` reducer failed with lazy materialization or parse errors;
  clang accepted the checked-in PA33 regression; focused PA33 regression and
  direct reducers pass; focused Boost `libs/bloom/test//test_bulk_operations`
  builds, links, and runs; full `libs/bloom/test` advances to 4 failed updates,
  with compile failures in `test_capacity`, `test_fpr`, and `test_insertion`
  at `std::array<decltype(fac()),10>` while analyzing
  `filter_core::capacity_for`, plus a `test_construction` runtime
  `std::bad_alloc`; PA33 report passes `304/304`; full direct-LowIR report
  passes `3111/3111`; full strict direct-LowIR compare passes with
  `CPPGM_BUILD_TEST_TIMEOUT_SEC=120`; perf check against
  `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json`
  passes (instructions `+0.56%`, RSS `+0.28%`, footprint `+1.35%`).
- `5b52e9726`: repaired the next Boost.Bloom static-member overload compile
  frontier. In `filter_core::capacity_for`, the unqualified static-member call
  `used_array_size(rng)` sees both a non-static `used_array_size() const` and
  the viable static `used_array_size(std::size_t)`. Overload analysis
  materialized implicit `this` for the non-static zero-argument candidate before
  cheaply rejecting it by argument count, which is invalid inside a static
  member function body. The fix rejects implicit-object candidates on arity and
  `this` availability before creating the implicit object, using the existing
  function-scope `this` binding as the signal and adding no new tracking
  structure. Validation: pre-fix no-STL reducer and focused Boost
  `libs/bloom/test//test_capacity` failed with `missing this binding`; clang
  accepted the PA15 reducer; invalid control `static int g(){ return f(); }`
  still rejects with no viable overload; focused PA15 regression passes; PA15
  report passes `143/143` in normal and direct-LowIR modes; PA15 placement audit
  passes; focused Boost `test_capacity` now compiles and links, then advances to
  runtime `std::bad_alloc`; full `libs/bloom/test` has 4 failed updates:
  `test_insertion` compile failure at `std::array<decltype(fac()),10>`, plus
  runtime `std::bad_alloc` in `test_capacity`, `test_construction`, and
  `test_fpr`; full direct-LowIR report passes `3112/3112`; full strict
  direct-LowIR compare passes with `CPPGM_BUILD_TEST_TIMEOUT_SEC=120`; perf
  check against
  `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json`
  passes (instructions `+0.13%`, RSS `+2.17%`, footprint `+1.36%`).
- d7150a985: repaired the next Boost.Bloom explicit type-argument compile
  frontier. `test_insertion` forms `std::array<decltype(fac()),10>` inside an
  instantiated function template, where `fac` is a local callable object.
  Structured `decltype` call analysis handled ordinary function names and
  function-pointer values for identifier callees, but did not fall through to
  the already-supported callable-object `operator()` path when the identifier
  resolved to a local value. The fix keeps the structured AST/type route and
  reuses existing member-call lookup only after the identifier callee resolves
  to a concrete named object type; it adds no source reparse or broad tracking
  table. Validation: pre-fix no-STL reducer and focused Boost
  `libs/bloom/test//test_insertion` failed with class-template instantiation
  failure at `array<decltype(fac()),10>`; clang accepted the reducer; focused
  PA22 regression passes; PA22 placement audit exits cleanly with existing
  backend-owner notes; PA22 report passes `443/443` in normal and direct-LowIR
  modes; focused Boost `test_insertion` compiles, links, and runs;
  full `libs/bloom/test` advances to 3 failed updates, all runtime
  `std::bad_alloc` aborts in `test_capacity`, `test_construction`, and
  `test_fpr`; full direct-LowIR report passes `3113/3113`; full strict
  direct-LowIR compare passes with `CPPGM_BUILD_TEST_TIMEOUT_SEC=120`; perf
  check against
  `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json`
  passes (instructions `+0.26%`, RSS `+1.07%`, footprint `+1.40%`).
- dffdaa46d: fixed the Boost.Bloom runtime `std::bad_alloc` frontier in
  `test_capacity`. `filter_core::capacity_for(100, 0.1)` returned an enormous
  capacity because native call lowering materialized XMM arguments sequentially:
  after `exp` returned in `xmm0`, the expression argument for `pow` lived in
  `xmm1` and the exponent lived in `xmm0`; call setup copied `xmm1` into
  `xmm0` before reading the second argument, so both `pow` arguments became the
  first value. The fix keeps this in the native backend by lowering XMM
  register arguments as a parallel copy, using a spare XMM register when one is
  available and reserving a single 16-byte call scratch slot only for all-XMM
  cycles with no spare register. Stack-passed float arguments are stored before
  the XMM shuffle, so delayed register materialization cannot corrupt them.
  Validation: focused reducer changed from `direct_pow=0.694842` to
  `direct=0.1 stored=0.1` and exits `0`; dumped MIR now uses `xmm2` to shuffle
  the `xmm1`/`xmm0` cycle before `pow`; focused PA32 regression passes; PA32
  report passes `65/65`; PA23 direct-LowIR report passes `103/103` after
  updating structural MIR refs for the intentional call-setup order/no-op
  drift; focused Boost `libs/bloom/test//test_capacity` passes; a full
  `libs/bloom/test` rerun advanced past `test_capacity` and then showed the
  next confirmed frontier in `test_construction`
  (`hash_function().state` remains `42` where Boost expects `-1`), while
  `test_fpr` became a CPU-bound long runtime and was stopped after the local
  stall cap; full direct-LowIR report passes `3114/3114`; strict direct-LowIR
  compare passes; perf check against
  `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json`
  passes (instructions `+0.23%`, RSS `+0.98%`, footprint `+1.37%`).
- `3d58fd644`: fixed the next Boost.Bloom runtime state frontier in
  `test_construction`. Defaulted move construction for `filter` was classified
  with the copy-triviality predicate, so the generated `filter(filter&&)` body
  used `copyobj` for an `empty_value<Hash>` base even when the selected
  `Hash(Hash&&)` constructor was nontrivial and reset the source state. The fix
  adds a structured move-triviality query beside the existing host-ABI copy
  query, uses it only for move-constructor trivial-lifecycle decisions, and
  applies the same move-aware predicate to synthesized move-constructor trivial
  field-prefix copies. No source reparse or broad tracking table is added.
  Validation: the reduced `stateful`/`empty_value`/`filter` probe now exits `0`
  and emitted LowIR calls the nontrivial base move constructor instead of
  copying storage; focused PA16 regression passes; PA16 direct-LowIR report
  passes `140/140`; PA16 placement audit marks the new reducer owner-reached
  with only expected semantic-owner notes; focused Boost
  `libs/bloom/test//test_construction` passes; full `libs/bloom/test` passes
  and updates 38 targets, including `test_fpr`; full direct-LowIR report passes
  `3115/3115`; strict direct-LowIR compare passes; perf check against
  `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json`
  passes (instructions `+0.28%`, RSS `+1.17%`, footprint `+1.35%`).
- `6c3d61942`: fixed the first Boost.Charconv compile frontier. The primary
  `dragon_box_print_chars<Float, FloatTraits>` function template takes
  `typename FloatTraits::carrier_uint`, while the explicit specializations in
  `to_chars.cpp` spell the resolved integer type in the parameter list. After
  explicit template arguments were substituted, the specialization matcher kept
  the structured dependent member type
  `dragonbox_float_traits<float>::carrier_uint` and compared it directly
  against `std::uint32_t`, so no function template matched and collection
  failed with `unknown function template explicit specialization`. The fix
  resolves only the substituted explicit-specialization function type before
  signature comparison, using the existing dependent-type resolver and adding
  no source reparse path or tracking table. Validation: the no-STL reducer
  failed before the fix and now compiles; PA22 regression
  `518-explicit-specialization-dependent-param-typedef.t` passes; PA22
  placement audit passes with `--fail-on-early`; PA22 direct-LowIR report
  passes `444/444`; direct Boost `to_chars.cpp` advances to
  `unsupported global array initializer for
  boost::charconv::detail::extended_cache_long_impl<true>::multiplier_index_info_table`;
  full `libs/charconv/test` rerun advances to that same next frontier with one
  failed update; full direct-LowIR report passes `3116/3116`; strict
  direct-LowIR compare passes after rerunning an isolated transient PA19 witness
  failure cleanly; perf check against
  `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json`
  passes (instructions `+0.29%`, RSS `+2.07%`, footprint `+1.37%`).
- `132484e04`: fixed the next Boost.Charconv `to_chars.o` static table
  frontier. `extended_cache_long_impl<true>::multiplier_index_info_table`
  instantiated as field assignment statements into a static constexpr array of
  nested aggregate class elements, and `main_cache_holder_impl<true>::cache`
  instantiated as constructor actions for constexpr `uint128` elements. LowIR
  global collection only handled scalar braced array data, so it rejected the
  aggregate assignment shape and then the constructor-action shape as
  unsupported global array initializers. The fix stays on structured semantic
  nodes: it overlays scalar field assignment data into global arrays, and for
  simple constructor-action arrays it inspects the resolved constructor body for
  `this->field = parameter/constant` assignments and substitutes the call
  arguments into static data. It does not reparse source text or add a broad
  tracking table, and it avoids generating large dynamic initializer call
  sequences for Boost's constexpr tables. Validation: both PA20 reducers pass
  and emit static global data; PA20 placement audit passes with
  `--fail-on-early`; PA20 direct-LowIR report passes `77/77`; direct Boost
  `to_chars.cpp` compiles; full `libs/charconv/test` rerun updates 148 targets
  with `to_chars.test` passing and one remaining `from_chars.test` runtime
  failure at the signed `__int128_t` numeric-limits assertion; full
  direct-LowIR report passes `3118/3118` at low concurrency; strict
  direct-LowIR compare passes; three-run perf check against
  `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json`
  passes (instructions `+0.27%`, RSS `+2.48%`, footprint `+1.37%`).
- `a6a9d8997`: fixed the Boost.Charconv `from_chars.test` signed `__int128_t`
  runtime frontier. libc++'s `numeric_limits<__int128_t>::max()` returns a
  static constexpr class member `__max`; cppgm had cached that member through
  the legacy `long long` `constant_value` field and folded it to
  `9223372036854775807`, so the Boost check against `unsigned long long` max
  failed. Simply loading the member was not valid because C++11 static
  constexpr integral members need no out-of-class storage definition for this
  use, so the fix inlines the already-structured initializer AST for int128
  static constexpr scalar members and leaves the normal non-int128 fast path
  unchanged. No source reparsing or broad tracking table is added. Validation:
  the no-STL `limits_like<__int128_t>::max()` reducer and hosted `<limits>`
  reducer fail before the fix and now run with exit `0`; PA33 regression
  `772-static-constexpr-int128-member-not-narrowed.t` passes; PA33 direct-LowIR
  report passes `305/305`; PA33 placement audit still exits nonzero on
  pre-existing PA33 cluster-early tests, with the new regression in the hosted
  int128 cluster; full `libs/charconv/test` passes and updates 150 targets
  with log `/tmp/boost-frontier-charconv-after-int128-static-constexpr-20260527.log`;
  full direct-LowIR report passes `3119/3119`; strict direct-LowIR compare
  passes; three-run perf check against
  `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json`
  passes (instructions `+0.31%`, RSS `+1.10%`, footprint `+1.37%`).
- this commit: restored the PA33 hosted `__is_nothrow_invocable` gate found
  while validating the Boost.Core `underlying_type` fix. The existing
  `700-hosted-nothrow-invocable-cache-default.t` reducer declares the reserved
  trait name as a reduced global stand-in for the hosted implementation, while
  PA22 still guards that user declarations inside `namespace std` are not
  treated as builtins. The structured bool trait evaluator now permits the
  reserved global form but keeps the existing standard-include check for
  `std::__is_invocable_r` / standard spellings. Validation: focused PA33
  reducer passes; PA22 negative guard
  `500-user-std-invocable-variable-template-not-builtin-bad.t` still passes;
  PA16/PA33 direct-LowIR report passes `1345/1345`; strict direct-LowIR compare
  passes.

Local Boost wrapper state:

- `/Users/vishvananda/boost_1_91_0/cppgm-b2-toolchain.sh` points at this
  worktree's `dev/cppgm++`.
- The wrapper routes C and assembly compile/preprocess actions to host clang,
  C++ compile/preprocess actions to `cppgm++`, and link actions to host clang++.
- Runtime Boost verification should use direct Boost.Build execution. If
  freshly linked Mach-O executables stall and unified logs report
  `AppleSystemPolicy` / `Security policy would not allow process`, enable the
  launching terminal application under macOS Privacy & Security -> Developer
  Tools and restart the terminal/Codex process tree before rerunning. Do not
  substitute a checked-in launcher for normal frontier validation.

## Sequential Process

1. Run Boost suites in the order listed in
   `docs/boost-b2-suite-status-20260511.md`.
2. Use one suite at a time with B2 compile parallelism, for example:

   ```sh
   cd /Users/vishvananda/boost_1_91_0
   JOBS=12 ./run-cppgm-b2.sh -a libs/accumulators/test
   ```

3. If the suite passes, mark it in this tracker and continue to the next suite.
4. If it fails, identify the first real compiler frontier, reduce it to the
   smallest useful source, and prefer a non-STL reducer when possible.
5. Place the regression in the earliest owning PA and first correct cluster.
   Use `ROADMAP.md`, PA READMEs, the reducer's essential assertion, and:

   ```sh
   python3 scripts/audit_pa_feature_placement.py --pa paNN \
     --markdown-out /tmp/boost-frontier-placement-paNN.md \
     --json-out /tmp/boost-frontier-placement-paNN.json \
     --fail-on-early
   ```

   Include every touched PA. Course tests for PA1-PA9 belong under
   `cppgm.tests/course/pa*` unless the reducer is a hosted C++ extension, in
   which case use the hosted compatibility PAs.
6. Before implementation changes, confirm the reducer fails on the current
   branch and record the pre-fix diagnostic here.
7. Fix the compiler in `dev/`, validate the focused regression, the owner PA
   report, strict LowIR compare when relevant, and the Boost target.
8. Run the perf gate against the baseline above before committing any compiler
   fix.
9. Commit each coherent fix with the regression, implementation, and tracker
   update before moving to the next unrelated frontier.

## Fix Ledger

| Status | Frontier seed | Root cause | Owner PA | Regression | Pre-fix result | Validation | Fix commit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| fixed | `libs/bloom/test//test_capacity`; `filter_core::capacity_for(100, 0.1)` evaluates `pow(1.0 - exp(-(double)2 / c), (double)2)` while the first argument is in `xmm1` and the second is in `xmm0`. | Native call lowering copied XMM arguments one at a time in argument order. For an argument shuffle such as `xmm1 -> xmm0` and `xmm0 -> xmm1`, the first copy clobbered the second source, so `pow` received the first argument twice and produced a huge capacity. The fix lowers XMM call arguments as a parallel copy, using a spare XMM register for cycles and a single 16-byte call scratch slot only when no spare exists; stack-passed float arguments and memory/immediate XMM arguments wait until their destination register is no longer needed as another argument source. | `pa32:200`; PA23 structural MIR refs updated for intentional call-setup order/no-op drift | `pa32/tests/general/200-host-f64-call-arg-shuffle.t`; PA23 structural refs `200-f64-direct-call`, `500-mixed-gpr-xmm-call-abi`, `600-indirect-mixed-gpr-xmm-call-abi`, and `700-call-setup-forwarding-no-preserve` | Focused reducer returned `direct_pow=0.694842` for the direct `pow` call while the stored-intermediate path returned `0.1`; Boost `test_capacity` aborted with uncaught `std::bad_alloc` after capacity overflow. | `make -C dev all -j12`; reducer now prints `direct=0.1 stored=0.1` and exits `0`; MIR dumps show scratch shuffling before `pow` and delayed memory-first materialization before a host `combine` call; focused PA32 regression passes; `ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa32' ... make test-report` passes `65/65`; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa23' ... make test-report` passes `103/103`; focused `JOBS=12 ./run-cppgm-b2.sh -a libs/bloom/test//test_capacity` passes; full Bloom rerun advances to `test_construction` state mismatch and `test_fpr` long runtime/stall; full direct-LowIR report passes `3114/3114`; strict direct-LowIR compare passes; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json` passes (`+0.23%` instructions, RSS `+0.98%`, footprint `+1.37%`). | same commit |
| fixed | `libs/bloom/test//test_construction`; moving `boost::bloom::filter` should move the stateful hash function and leave the source hash state at `-1`. | Defaulted move constructors were using the copy-triviality predicate for trivial lifecycle classification. A class whose copy construction is storage-trivial but whose selected move constructor is nontrivial was therefore lowered as `copyobj`, skipping source-state reset. The same copy-triviality shortcut was also used by synthesized move-constructor leading field-prefix copies. The fix adds a structured host-ABI move-triviality predicate and uses it for move constructor lifecycle/prefix decisions without reparsing source text or adding broad state tracking. | `pa16:300` | `pa16/tests/spec/300-defaulted-move-nontrivial-subobject.t`; direct-LowIR ref refreshes for `pa16/tests/spec/300-moveonly-defaulted-move.ref` and `pa18/tests/general/300-defaulted-move-constructor-reference-member.ref` after newly trivial defaulted moves lower directly to `copyobj`. | The reduced `stateful`/`empty_value`/`filter` probe returned failure because `f1.get().state` remained `42`; emitted LowIR for `filter(filter&&)` used `copyobj` for the `empty_value` base instead of calling the nontrivial `stateful(stateful&&)` base-entry constructor. Boost `test_construction` failed `f1.hash_function().state == -1` with observed state `42`. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j12`; reduced probe exits `0` and emitted LowIR calls the nontrivial base move path; focused PA16 regression passes; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa16' ... make test-report` passes `140/140`; PA16 placement audit exits cleanly with the new reducer owner-reached; focused `JOBS=12 ./run-cppgm-b2.sh -a libs/bloom/test//test_construction` passes; full `libs/bloom/test` passes and updates 38 targets; full direct-LowIR report passes `3115/3115`; strict direct-LowIR compare passes; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json` passes (`+0.28%` instructions, RSS `+1.17%`, footprint `+1.35%`). | this commit |
| fixed | `libs/charconv/test` `to_chars.o`; Boost.Dragonbox emits large `static constexpr` tables including `extended_cache_long_impl<true>::multiplier_index_info_table` and `main_cache_holder_impl<true>::cache`. | LowIR global array collection only handled scalar braced initializer data. Template-instantiated constexpr aggregate class arrays arrived as field assignment statements into `array[index].field`, and constexpr class arrays with user-provided constructors arrived as constructor actions. The fix collects both shapes from structured `CallSemNode` data: aggregate field assignments are sorted into static global data by byte offset, and simple constructor-action arrays are folded to static data by reading the resolved constructor body's `this->field = parameter/constant` assignments and substituting the call arguments. No source reparsing or broad tracking table is added, and large Boost constexpr tables avoid dynamic initializer calls. | `pa20:300` | `pa20/tests/spec/301-static-constexpr-aggregate-class-array-member.t`; `pa20/tests/spec/302-static-constexpr-constructor-class-array-member.t` | The aggregate reducer failed with `unsupported global array initializer`; after that path was covered, direct Boost `to_chars.cpp` advanced to `unsupported global array initializer for boost::charconv::detail::main_cache_holder_impl<true>::cache`. The constructor reducer compiled through a dynamic fallback attempt but returned the wrong value because the original hidden `this` argument was treated as constructor data, confirming the static collector was needed. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j12`; both reducers compile, link, run, and emit static global data; focused PA20 checks pass; PA20 placement audit passes with `--fail-on-early`; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa20' ... make test-report-nobuild` passes `77/77`; direct Boost `to_chars.cpp` compiles; full `libs/charconv/test` rerun updates 148 targets with `to_chars.test` passing and one remaining `from_chars.test` runtime failure at the signed `__int128_t` numeric-limits assertion; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 STRICT_PAS='pa18 pa19 pa21 pa22' ... make test-strict-nobuild` passes; reliable full `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 TEST_REPORT_ASSIGNMENT_JOBS=1 TEST_REPORT_SUBTEST_JOBS=4 CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1 make test-report-nobuild` passes `3118/3118`; three-run perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json` passes (`+0.27%` instructions, RSS `+2.48%`, footprint `+1.37%`). | this commit |
| fixed | `libs/charconv/test//from_chars.test`; signed `__int128_t` numeric-limits check expects `std::numeric_limits<__int128_t>::max()` to be greater than `unsigned long long` max. | Static constexpr int128 class members were folded through the legacy `long long` `constant_value` cache, so libc++ `__libcpp_numeric_limits<__int128_t, true>::__max` became `LLONG_MAX`. Loading the member instead would create an undefined static-member storage reference, so the fix inlines the structured initializer AST only for int128 static constexpr scalar members and leaves the ordinary non-int128 cache path unchanged. No source reparsing or broad tracking table is added. | `pa34:700` | `pa34/tests/compile/772-static-constexpr-int128-member-not-narrowed.t` | Hosted `<limits>` reducer returned failure because `numeric_limits<__int128_t>::max() > ULLONG_MAX` was false; no-STL `limits_like<__int128_t>::max()` returned high bits `0`, and LowIR for `max()` returned `i128 9223372036854775807`. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j12`; hosted and no-STL reducers run with exit `0`; focused PA34 regression passes; PA34 direct-LowIR report passes `305/305`; PA34 placement audit still exits nonzero on pre-existing PA34 cluster-early tests, with the new regression in cluster 700; full `libs/charconv/test` passes and updates 150 targets; full direct-LowIR report passes `3119/3119`; strict direct-LowIR compare passes; three-run perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json` passes (`+0.31%` instructions, RSS `+1.10%`, footprint `+1.37%`). | this commit |
| fixed | `libs/chrono/test//cycle_count_h.test` and `runtime_resolution_h.test`; local `typedef cycle_count<...> clock;` is followed by qualified type names such as `clock::duration` while system headers also provide an outer value named `clock`. | The AST parser's declaration-start heuristic rejected any qualified-name declaration candidate whose leading identifier was visible as a value, so an outer `clock()` value beat the nearer block-scope typedef and `clock::duration` was not parsed as a type. The fix reuses the existing nearest-scope `unqualified_identifier_prefers_value_name` decision for the qualified-name lookahead, so no new tracking table or source reparsing is added. | `pa10:300` | `pa10/tests/general/300-local-typedef-shadows-value-qualified-type.t` | The PA10 reducer failed with `ERROR: expected declaration near KW_VOID:void`, and direct Boost `cycle_count.cpp` / `runtime_resolution.cpp` failed at the same parser frontier before reaching semantic analysis. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j12`; focused PA10 regression passes; PA10 direct-LowIR report passes `129/129`; PA10 placement audit still exits nonzero on pre-existing syntax/semantic-owner findings and flags the new syntax reducer as `class.basic`; direct Boost `cycle_count.cpp` now advances to Boost.MPL `and_` default-argument binding, and `runtime_resolution.cpp` advances to a duration constructor-conversion frontier; full `libs/chrono/test` rerun updates 288 targets and fails 33 remaining compile updates with log `/tmp/boost-frontier-chrono-after-local-typedef-shadow-20260527.log`; focused PA9 rerun after an earlier noisy timeout passes `11/11`; clean full direct-LowIR report passes `3120/3120`; strict direct-LowIR compare passes; three-run perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json` passes (`+0.31%` instructions, RSS `+2.10%`, footprint `+1.40%`). | this commit |
| fixed | `libs/chrono/test//two_obj_p_d.test`; Boost.Chrono output formatting calls hosted `std::num_put<char, std::ostreambuf_iterator<char>>::put` while printing a duration through `duration_put::put_value`. | The generated hosted `num_put` bridge expects a pointer to the `ostreambuf_iterator` storage so it can pass the iterator's streambuf pointer to the host virtual `do_put` call. The emitter instead indexed eight bytes past the already-materialized direct-object slot, so the bridge loaded a bogus iterator value and libc++ jumped through invalid formatting state. The fix keeps the existing bridge ABI and passes `addr $argobj` for direct-object iterator slots; it adds no new tracking table, allocation, or source reparsing. | `pa35:700` | `pa35/tests/link/700-hosted-num-put-ostreambuf-iterator-runtime-smoke.t` | A direct hosted reducer using `std::use_facet<std::num_put<char, std::ostreambuf_iterator<char>>>(std::cout.getloc()).put(...)` linked but crashed with `Bus error: 10`; LLDB showed the crash under `cppgm_host_num_put_char_put_long_long` while `two_obj_p_d` failed the same way. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j12`; direct hosted reducer prints `60`; focused PA35 reducer passes; PA35 placement audit passes with `--fail-on-early`; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa35' ... make test-report-nobuild` passes `125/125`; focused B2 `libs/chrono/test//two_obj_p_d` passes; full `libs/chrono/test` advances to 6 failed updates with log `/tmp/boost-frontier-chrono-after-numput-20260527.log`; strict direct-LowIR compare passes; three-run perf rerun against `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json` passes (`+0.29%` instructions, RSS `+1.12%`, footprint `+1.41%`) after an initial noisy RSS-only miss. | this commit |
| fixed | `libs/chrono/test//two_obj_p_h.test`; Boost.Chrono header-only clock definitions use `BOOST_CHRONO_INLINE` on in-class static member declarations while the out-of-class definitions in the header do not repeat `inline`. | Class member function binding did not carry a structured inline bit from declarations to later definitions. The out-of-class static member definitions therefore upgraded to strong external linkage in every including object, and the linker rejected duplicate Boost.Chrono clock symbols. The fix preserves `is_inline` on the existing function binding and class-function option paths, merges it across declaration/definition registration, and uses that bit when upgrading/emitting symbol linkage. This is a single binding flag with no source reparsing or broad side table. | `pa32:100` | `pa32/tests/general/100-inline-static-member-outdef-header-duplicate.t` | The PA32 reducer linked two objects that both included `static inline int value();` followed by an out-of-class header definition; before the fix both objects emitted strong `InlineStaticMemberOutdef::value()` and the link failed with a duplicate symbol. Focused Boost `two_obj_p_h` failed similarly with duplicate Boost.Chrono clock definitions. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j12`; focused PA32 reducer passes; existing `pa32/tests/general/100-inline-header-duplicate.t` passes; existing `pa35/tests/link/700-hosted-inline-header-odr-link-smoke.t` passes; PA32 placement audit passes with `--fail-on-early`; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa32' ... make test-report-nobuild` passes `66/66`; PA35 direct-LowIR report passes `125/125`; strict direct-LowIR compare passes; focused B2 `libs/chrono/test//two_obj_p_h` passes; full `libs/chrono/test` advances to 5 failed updates with log `/tmp/boost-frontier-chrono-after-inline-outdef-20260527.log`; three-run perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json` passes (`+0.19%` instructions, RSS `+1.61%`, footprint `+1.38%`). | this commit |
| fixed | `libs/chrono/test//test_7868_d.test`, `libs/chrono/test//test_7868_h.test`, and `libs/chrono/test//time_point_input_h.test`; Boost.Chrono time-point input uses `goto exit;` inside `BOOST_TRY`, with the `exit:` label after `BOOST_CATCH_END`. | `goto_statement` LowIR lowering jumped directly to the target block without unwinding active cleanup scopes. For a goto out of a host-EH try block, the same label could also be reached after `eh_end`, so the object backend observed inconsistent host-EH region stacks for the target. The fix records per-body label cleanup depths only for structured `CallSemNode` subtrees that contain goto or labels, then reuses existing cleanup emission before the jump. It adds no source reparsing and keeps the tracking local to the function or initializer being lowered. | `pa33:100` | `pa33/tests/general/100-host-eh-goto-out-of-try.t` | Focused Boost targets failed with `ERROR: inconsistent host EH region stack at ...$^goto_17`; the reducer lowered a jump from inside the try to the label without first ending the host-EH cleanup region. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j12`; focused PA33 reducer passes; PA33 placement audit passes with `--fail-on-early`; reducer LowIR now emits `eh_end` before `jump ^goto_4`; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa33' ... make test-report-nobuild` passes `84/84`; strict direct-LowIR compare passes; focused B2 `test_7868_d`, `test_7868_h`, and `time_point_input_h` pass; full `libs/chrono/test` reaches zero failed updates with log `/tmp/boost-frontier-chrono-after-goto-try-20260527.log`, while B2 still reports four expected-fail bookkeeping skips; three-run perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json` passes (`+0.21%` instructions, RSS `+0.45%`, footprint `+1.41%`). | this commit |
| fixed | `libs/chrono/test//implicit_constructot_fail` and `libs/chrono/test//time_point_implicit_f`; Boost.Chrono verifies that `duration` and `time_point` constructors marked `explicit` do not participate in ordinary copy-initialization. | Assignment-form class initialization only selected non-explicit constructors for copy-list-initialization; ordinary `T x = expr` still fell through to the direct-initialization profile and accepted explicit constructors. The fix uses the existing structured `initializer::uses_assignment_form` bit to select the non-explicit construction profile for ordinary copy-initialization, while preserving the same-type function-style prvalue path such as `T x = T(...)` by reusing the already computed constructor-argument shape. | fixed | Boost.Container `stable_vector_test`; `boost/container/detail/mutex.hpp` probes and calls GNU legacy sync-lock builtins. | Hosted builtin support did not advertise, analyze, or lower `__sync_lock_test_and_set` and `__sync_lock_release`, so Boost's lock wrapper fell through as unknown functions. The first perf check was narrowly over the instruction gate (`+1.02%`), so the builtin-call dispatcher now has a cheap double-underscore guard for ordinary non-builtin callees; this avoids a broad tracking table or source reparsing and brings the guarded implementation under the perf budget. | PA34 | `pa34/tests/compile/600-gnu-sync-lock-builtins.t` | Focused Boost build-only `stable_vector_test` stopped with `ERROR: unknown function __sync_lock_test_and_set` in `boost/container/detail/mutex.hpp`. | fixed | Boost.Container `scoped_allocator_adaptor_test`; unqualified explicit-template-id `get<I>(tuple)` found libc++'s declaration-only hidden friend through ADL but output had to emit the namespace `std::get` definition with its ABI tag. | Output binding resolution stopped at the ADL-selected friend declaration. That declaration had the right semantic namespace and template arguments but no body and no ABI tag, while the namespace-scope body template was not materialized unless the call was written as `std::get<I>(...)`. The fix is limited to declaration-only function-template bindings during output: require existing structured semantic qualified-name metadata, look for an already-defined same-name exact-type function, otherwise instantiate only same-shape function-template body declarations from that namespace with the already-known template arguments and then exact-type verify the materialized binding. | fixed | Boost.Container `new_delete_resource_test`; `libs/container/test/new_delete_resource_test.cpp` contains a raw Windows-1252 em dash byte (`0x97`) in a `//` comment. | Source decoding rejected every invalid UTF-8 lead byte before whitespace/comment skipping could discard the comment. The fix keeps the ASCII and valid-UTF-8 paths unchanged, maps only defined Windows-1252 C1 compatibility bytes on the existing invalid-non-ASCII slow path, and still rejects bytes such as `0xff`. | fixed | Boost.Container `static_vector_test`; full-expression class temporaries passed as call arguments must be destroyed if the callee throws before returning. | LowIR call-unwind wrapper selection ran before argument lowering, so a call whose only cleanup was registered by an argument temporary could emit the normal full-expression destructor after the call but no host-EH cleanup edge covering the throwing call. Boost.Move conversion shapes also hid some materialized temporaries under the argument subtree. The fix uses typed argument/parameter metadata and a guarded structured argument-subtree scan, then opens the existing shared host-unwind region immediately before emitting a call once argument cleanups are actually registered. It adds no source reparsing or persistent cleanup table. | PA33 | `pa33/tests/general/200-host-eh-call-argument-temporary-cleanup.t`; PA18 LowIR refs refreshed for expected cleanup regions in `100-required-template-special-member-emission`, `300-reference-prvalue-template-member-temp-cleanup`, and `300-template-array-reference-cv-default-arg`. | Focused no-STL reducers `/tmp/cppgm-boost-reducers-20260528/static_vector_capacity0_leak.cpp`, `/tmp/cppgm-boost-reducers-20260528/throwing_call_arg_temp_cleanup.cpp`, and `/tmp/cppgm-boost-reducers-20260528/static_vector_insert_temp_leak.cpp` leaked temporaries on throwing call paths; focused Boost direct runtime `libs/container/test//static_vector_test` failed six `counting_value::count() == 0` checks at `static_vector_test.cpp:784-808`. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; focused PA33 regression passes; PA33 placement audit passes with `--fail-on-early`; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa33' ORDERED=false TEST_REPORT_SUBTEST_JOBS=8 TEST_REPORT_ASSIGNMENT_JOBS=1 make test-report-nobuild` passes `85/85`; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1 STRICT_SUBTEST_JOBS=8 make test-strict-nobuild` passes all strict PAs after the PA18 reference refresh; focused Boost `libs/container/test//static_vector_test` passes; three-run perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-call-arg-cleanup-8637a8665-20260528.json` passes: instructions `+0.52%`, RSS `+0.55%`, footprint `-0.01%`, log `/tmp/cppgm-perf-after-call-arg-cleanup-check-20260528.log`. | this commit |
| fixed | Boost.Container `devector_test`; Boost.Move must detect `noex_copy` as nothrow-copy-constructible so the test takes the nonthrowing copy path. | CPPGM did not treat `defined __is_identifier` as true and did not answer Clang-style `__is_identifier(__is_nothrow_constructible)` queries. Boost.Move therefore selected its weaker `__has_extension`-only trait detection path, missed the available `__is_nothrow_constructible` builtin, and fell back to `is_pod<T>::value`; `noex_copy` looked like a potentially throwing copy type and the runtime test deliberately threw. The fix implements Clang-compatible `__is_identifier` preprocessor query handling for keywords, ordinary identifiers, and supported type-trait builtins, and syncs the advertised `__has_builtin` type-trait names with the semantic builtin surface. It adds no source reparsing. | PA34:600 | `pa34/tests/compile/600-clang-is-identifier-type-trait-detection.t` | Probe `/tmp/cppgm-boost-reducers-20260528/clang_defined_builtin_probe.cpp` failed because `defined __is_identifier` evaluated false under cppgm; Boost-shaped reducer `/tmp/cppgm-boost-reducers-20260528/boost_move_noex_copy_trait.cpp` returned `1` under cppgm while clang returned `0`; focused Boost `libs/container/test//devector_test` terminated with uncaught `boost::container::test_exception`. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; direct `clang_defined_builtin_probe.cpp` and `boost_move_noex_copy_trait.cpp` reducers pass; focused PA34 regression passes; host clang accepts the new PA34 fixture with `-x c++`; PA34 placement audit shows only pre-existing early-cluster findings and does not flag the new `600` test; full PA34 owner report is blocked by unrelated `700-hosted-nothrow-invocable-cache-default.t`, which also fails under host clang; default strict direct-LowIR compare passes; focused Boost `devector_test` passes; full Boost.Container runtime passes and updates 325 targets; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-7384ec2ae-20260528.json` passes: instructions `+0.19%`, RSS `+0.98%`, footprint `-0.07%`, log `/tmp/cppgm-perf-after-is-identifier-check-20260528.log`. | this commit |
| fixed | Boost.Core `bit_countl_test`, `bit_floor_test`, `bit_width_test`, and the corresponding constexpr bitops targets for small unsigned operands. | Fixed GNU bit builtins `__builtin_clz`, `__builtin_ctz`, and `__builtin_popcount` were evaluated and lowered with the original argument width instead of their declared promoted unsigned parameter width; generic Clang `__builtin_*g` builtins keep the source operand width. `__builtin_clz((unsigned char)0x31)` therefore returned `2` instead of `26`, Boost subtracted the 24-bit promotion delta, and `bit_width` / `bit_floor` computed invalid results for small unsigned types. The fix keeps the structured builtin parameter type on the call node and makes constexpr evaluation plus LowIR lowering use the fixed GNU operand type without reparsing source text. | PA34 constexpr hosted builtin semantics; PA35 runtime hosted builtin lowering. | `pa34/tests/compile/600-host-builtin-bitops-widths.t`; `pa35/tests/link/700-hosted-builtin-bitops-promote-runtime-smoke.t` | Clang accepted and ran `/tmp/cppgm-boost-reducers-20260528/bit_floor_small_unsigned.cpp`, while cppgm returned exit `244`; emitted LowIR used `u8` slots and shifts by `7` for `__builtin_clz((unsigned char)0x31)`, matching the Boost runtime zero result. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; reducer now runs with exit `0` and LowIR zero-extends to `u32`; focused PA34 and PA35 regressions pass, and host clang accepts the PA34 fixture; PA35 owner report passes `127/127`; clean root strict direct-LowIR compare passes, with post-cleanup log `/tmp/cppgm-strict-after-bitops-promote-cleanup-20260528.log`; PA34 owner report remains blocked by the known unrelated `700-hosted-nothrow-invocable-cache-default.t` failure at `1188/1189`, and PA34 placement audit still reports only pre-existing early-cluster hosted-builtin findings, not this new `600` test; focused Boost bitops B2 target passes with log `/tmp/boost-frontier-core-bitops-after-cleanup-20260528.log`; full Boost.Core rerun advances to 11 remaining failed targets with log `/tmp/boost-frontier-core-after-bitops-promote-20260528.log`; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-7384ec2ae-20260528.json` passes: instructions `+0.60%`, RSS `+1.52%`, footprint `-0.08%`, log `/tmp/cppgm-perf-after-bitops-promote-cleanup-check-20260528.log`. | this commit |
| fixed | Boost.Core `sp_thread_pause_test`, `yield_prim_pthread_cancel_test`, and `yield_prim_windows_h_test`; Boost.Core `yield_primitives.hpp` expands `sp_thread_pause()` to `__asm__ __volatile__("rep; nop" : : : "memory")` on this host. | Semantic analysis preserved only the GNU asm qualifiers and dropped the parsed asm clauses, while LowIR lowering rejected every `asm-statement`. The fix carries parsed asm clauses through CallSem and lowers only no-output/no-input spin/yield/nop-style GNU asm statements to `atomic_signal_fence 5`; asm with outputs, inputs, `goto`, or other templates remains unsupported instead of being silently discarded. | fixed | Boost.DateTime `testwrapping_int`; `wrapping_int<short, 10>::subtract(-8)` computes both `v % wrap_val` and `-(v / wrap_val)` through signed 32-bit arithmetic before a member call. | Native object lowering fed `idivq` with narrow integer operands that had not been normalized to the 64-bit execution width. In object mode, loading an `i32` negative value into `%eax` zero-extended it to a positive 64-bit dividend before `cqto`, and a narrow negative divisor would have had the same problem in `%rcx`. The fix normalizes both `%rax` and `%rcx` from the structured LowIR type immediately before signed or unsigned division/modulo. It adds no source or type reparsing. | PA32 host object/runtime | `pa32/tests/general/200-signed-i32-divmod-object-runtime-smoke.t` | Minimal object-mode reducer `scratch/member_call_after_mod_no_store_reducer.cpp` matched clang with exit `1` only after the fix; before the fix clang exited `1` while cppgm exited `205`. | fixed | Boost.DateTime `testgreg_month`; `greg_month::as_short_string()` and `as_long_string()` define function-local `static const char * const names[NumMonths]` arrays with one omitted pointer element. | Synthesized zero-fill pointer elements from array initialization reached global/static LowIR data emission as literal pointer-typed `nullptr` nodes. The global initializer code recognized `nullptr_t` and integral zero, but not pointer-typed literal `nullptr`, so it printed `ptr nullptr`; the LowIR parser requires `ptr addr @symbol` for pointer data and rejects null pointer data unless it is emitted as `zero 8`. | fixed | Boost.Coroutine `test_asymmetric_coroutine.cpp` and `test_symmetric_coroutine.cpp`; coroutine tests redeclare `coro::...::iterator` after a local object named `coro`. | The parser's declaration-start heuristic rejected named decl-specifier candidates when the first identifier had a nearer value binding. In Boost.Coroutine, the first qualifier is also a namespace alias, and `coro::...` remains a valid nested-name-specifier even after declaring a local variable named `coro`. The fix allows `identifier::` to start a qualified type/namespace candidate regardless of value-name preference, while keeping the `identifier<...>` template-id ambiguity guarded by the existing value/type heuristic. | fixed | Boost.Coroutine `test_asymmetric_coroutine.cpp`; Boost.Exception safe-bool returns `ptr_ ? &impl::get : 0`, where `impl::get` has type `T* get() const` and the target typedef is `T* (impl::*)() const`. | Declarator CV association checked pending pointer prefixes before pending function suffixes. For a member-function pointer returning a pointer, `D const * (C::*)() const` attached the trailing `const` to the return pointer instead of to the function suffix, storing a target type shaped as `function of () returning const pointer` instead of `function of () const returning pointer`. Constructor-unwind dispatch metadata now follows the shared host cleanup region when needed. | fixed | Boost.Core `underlying_type.run`; `boost::underlying_type<enum class E : unsigned short>::type` and the scoped-enum compatibility macro should expose the explicit enum base type. | `__underlying_type` accepted named enum types but always returned `int`. Enum parsing already computed size/alignment from the explicit base type, but discarded the structured base `TypePtr`, so hosted type traits could not recover `unsigned char` / `unsigned short` without reparsing. The fix stores the parsed enum underlying type on the named enum type and returns it from `__underlying_type`, falling back to `int` only when no metadata is present. Builtin `std::__1::align_val_t` also records its `size_t` base. | fixed | Boost.CRC `quick`, `pr15_test`, and `crc_test`; `make_partial_xor_products_table` initializes `std::array<Register, N> result = { 0 };`. | Positional aggregate initialization only synthesized brace-elided sublists for aggregate or array fields when they consumed more than one initializer clause. The fix lets array and nested aggregate fields synthesize a one-clause braced list after preserving direct-initialization cases such as same-aggregate object copies and string-literal array initialization. | fixed | Boost.Core `type_name_test`; `type_name<void() &>`, `type_name<void() const &>`, pack-expanded class names, function argument class names, and cv-qualified pointer partials must keep their structured type identity. | Three type-name frontiers were masking each other. Class partial-specialization matching stripped top-level cv from actual composite types even when the pattern did not spell cv, so `holder<T*>` could match `B const* volatile` and tie a cv-qualified pointer partial. Pack expansion for explicit function-template type arguments cloned only text, so `add_each<T>()...` could resolve the printed `A` through a local typedef instead of the already-bound class type. Finally, the structured function type model carried function cv but not `&` / `&&`, so `holder<void() &>` collapsed to `holder<void>`. The fix preserves actual cv during class-partial deduction, carries `resolved_type` through cloned and synthesized pack argument syntax, makes the fast explicit-argument resolver prefer that structured type, and adds a ref-qualifier field to function types, spelling, equality, hashing, template matching, and mangle keys. | fixed | Boost.Core `ref_ct_test`; `ref_test<int const>(boost::ref(ci))` must deduce through the lvalue `ref(T&)` overload and avoid the deleted `ref(T const&&)` overload. | Ordinary id-expression lookup set `allow_constant_fold=true` before returning local or namespace variable bindings, so `int const value = 0;` in ordinary expression analysis became a PRVALUE literal instead of an lvalue. Overload resolution could then bind the expression to `const&&`, reject the lvalue path, or choose the deleted Boost.Ref overload. The fix keeps ordinary variable id-expressions as lvalues and only enables this expression-side constant folding for pure constant-only bindings and class static members that already require the static-member constant path. | fixed | Boost.ContainerHash `hash_integral_test`; `boost::is_signed<wchar_t>` should be true on this host and `boost::is_signed<char32_t>` should be false, so the identity hash test chooses the same signed/unsigned branch as clang. | CPPGM modeled `wchar_t` as an unsigned 32-bit fundamental even though host clang reports `__WCHAR_TYPE__ int`, and the constexpr evaluator plus semantic conversion helper promoted `char32_t` to `int` even though `int` cannot represent all `char32_t` values. Boost.TypeTraits therefore computed the wrong signedness for both wide character types: `wchar_t` hashed through an unsigned conversion while the test expected signed `size_t` conversion, and `char32_t` was classified as signed by `(char32_t)-1 > (char32_t)0` evaluating false in constexpr. The fix makes the built-in `wchar_t` model signed, removes it from unsigned integral traits, and keeps the semantic and constexpr integral-promotion helpers aligned for `wchar_t` and `char32_t`. It uses existing type metadata with no source reparsing. | PA19:100 | `pa19/tests/general/100-char32-constant-promotion-unsigned.t` | Focused reducer `/tmp/cppgm-boost-reducers-20260528/char32_unsigned_promotion.cpp` failed its `static_assert` under cppgm while clang accepted it; full ContainerHash runtime failed only `hash_integral_test.run` with 256 `char32_t` identity mismatches, then focused rerun after the first promotion fix failed 128 `wchar_t` identity mismatches. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; reducer and clang control pass; focused PA19 regression passes; PA19 placement audit passes with `--fail-on-early`; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa19' ORDERED=false TEST_REPORT_SUBTEST_JOBS=8 TEST_REPORT_ASSIGNMENT_JOBS=1 make test-report-nobuild` passes `111/111`; default strict direct-LowIR compare passes; focused Boost `libs/container_hash/test//hash_integral_test` passes; full Boost.ContainerHash runtime passes and updates 319 targets; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-7384ec2ae-20260528.json` passes: instructions `+0.19%`, RSS `+0.06%`, footprint `-0.02%`, log `/tmp/cppgm-perf-after-wchar-char32-promotion-check-20260528.log`. | this commit |
| fixed | Boost.Container `map_test`; `boost::container::test::expected_pair[i]` placement-new writes in the emplace checks must write into the pointed-to `pair_storage`, not into the `expected_pair` global variable itself. | LowIR array-base lowering treated every global id-expression as an array object and emitted `addr @global` plus decay. For global pointer variables, `p[i]` therefore indexed from the storage address of the pointer object instead of the pointer value loaded from that object. In `map_test`, writes through `expected_pair[i]` overwrote the `expected_pair` pointer and following globals, corrupting the adaptive-pool singleton state before process teardown. The fix routes global subscript bases through the existing structured rvalue path, so global arrays still decay to storage, global pointers load their stored pointer, and references keep their existing behavior. It adds no source reparsing, persistent tracking table, or broad side state. | PA14 | `pa14/tests/general/200-global-pointer-array-subscript-load.t` | Scalar reducer `/tmp/cppgm-boost-reducers-20260528/global_pointer_subscript_scalar.cpp` returned `1` under cppgm while clang returned `0`; pre-fix LowIR showed `addr @cursor` / `unary decay ptr` for `cursor[1]`. Boost-shaped reducer `/tmp/cppgm-boost-reducers-20260528/map_after_adaptive_expected_only.cpp` wrote through `addr @expected_pair`, and focused Boost `libs/container/test//map_test` aborted in `adaptive_node_pool_impl.hpp:959` with `n_free_nodes == m_totally_free_blocks`. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j8`; scalar and Boost-shaped reducers pass, and the Boost-shaped LowIR now loads `@boost__container__test__expected_pair` before indexing; focused PA14 regression passes; PA14 placement audit passes with `--fail-on-early`; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa14' ORDERED=false TEST_REPORT_SUBTEST_JOBS=8 TEST_REPORT_ASSIGNMENT_JOBS=1 make test-report-nobuild` passes `60/60`; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 CPPGM_SKIP_DEV_REBUILD=1 STRICT_SUBTEST_JOBS=8 make test-strict-nobuild` passes; focused Boost `libs/container/test//map_test` passes; full Container runtime now fails only `devector_test.run`; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-9584a3460-20260528.json` passes: instructions `-0.03%`, RSS `+0.80%`, footprint `+0.01%`, log `/tmp/cppgm-perf-after-global-pointer-subscript-check-20260528.log`. | this commit |
| fixed | Boost.ContainerHash `libs/container_hash/examples/template.cpp`; `prime_fmod_size<void>::inv_sizes32` and `prime_fmod_size<void>::positions` are odr-used from `prime_fmod_size<void>::position(hash, size_index)` and require emitted out-of-class static data definitions. | The template declaration collector classified `constexpr std::size_t (*prime_fmod_size<T>::positions[])(std::size_t);` as an out-of-class member function because it treated any descendant `parameter-clause` as method-like, including the pointee function signature inside `(*positions[])(...)`. After that was corrected, early reference-member collection could still miss `positions`, mark the class template's stored static definitions as applied, and prevent the existing later static-member use path from retrying. The fix uses a cheap AST-shape test for whether a declarator's parameter clause belongs to the declared entity, and leaves stored out-of-class static definitions retryable only when a member is missing before class completion. | fixed | Boost.ContainerHash `libs/container_hash/examples/template.cpp`; `prime_fmod_size<void>::positions` is a `constexpr static` array of function pointers initialized with function template-ids such as `position<29, sizes[29]>`. | Typed constexpr initialization of function-pointer array elements only handled explicit `&function` addresses, so direct function designators could fall through to ordinary constant-expression evaluation. | fixed | Boost.Container `scoped_allocator_adaptor_test`; `dtl::dispatch_uses_allocator(..., piecewise_construct_t, boost::tuples::tuple<int, null_type, ...>, boost::tuples::tuple<int, null_type, ...>)` should select the fixed Boost.Tuple overload rather than tie with the variadic template-template fallback. | Function-template partial ordering did not build a usable transformed placeholder for template-template parameters, and substitution of a dependent template-template replacement did not preserve structured argument metadata. The fix creates dependent class-template placeholders for template-template parameters, preserves dependent template-template argument metadata through substitution, recurses that metadata in the existing placeholder-specificity scan, and only on equal instantiated function types uses a cheap source-pattern scan to prefer the overload whose function-parameter pattern does not depend on type parameter packs. | fixed | Boost.Container `pair_test`; nested `dtl::pair<movable_int, movable_int>` construction through `piecewise_construct` should select the forwarding constructor template over `pair(const T1&, const T2&)`. | Constructor selection returned immediately when ordinary non-template constructors produced an all-exact match, before arity-compatible constructor templates participated. For rvalue arguments, `pair(const T1&, const T2&)` was considered exact and its body then tried to copy move-only values. The fix keeps the exact-match shortcut for the common case, but defers it only when the selected exact ordinary constructor binds a non-lvalue through an lvalue-reference parameter and the class has an arity-compatible constructor template. The first broad arity scan failed the perf gate, so the final guard uses the existing analyzed source argument/category and parameter type before scanning templates. | fixed | Boost.Container `pair_test`; `boost::container::dtl::pair<int, float>` and related pair constructors accept `piecewise_construct_t, boost::tuples::tuple<...>, boost::tuples::tuple<...>` through a fixed-arity template-template parameter with defaulted `boost::tuples::null_type` tail arguments. | Function-template deduction had structured class-template-id metadata for the actual `boost::tuples::tuple<>`, but the dependent pattern's non-dependent default-tail arguments were compared by preserved text when no structured pattern argument was aligned. That made rooted `::boost::tuples::null_type` fail against canonical `boost::tuples::null_type`, so deduction deferred both tuple parameters and never bound the `BoostTuple` template-template parameter. The related one-argument constructor fast-filter also treated a direct template-template parameter head as a concrete template name and could drop viable constructor templates before deduction. The fix resolves only qualified mismatched non-template-id type arguments through existing semantic type lookup and feeds the resulting `TypePtr`s back into normal deduction; the fast-filter now lets direct template-template parameter heads through to deduction. It adds no source reparsing, persistent tracking table, or cache expansion. | PA18 | `pa18/tests/general/300-template-template-qualified-default-arg-deduction.t` | Minimal three-argument reducer failed with `deduce-template-arg-text-shape-fail pattern=::ns::marker actual=ns::marker` and `missing-template-template-parameter name=Tuple`; the one-argument reducer was dropped by `ctor-fast-filter reason=template-base-mismatch`. | fixed | Boost.Container `set_test`; `boost::container::set::erase(const key_type&)` redeclares the same signature imported from the tree base with `using base_t::erase`. | Visible member-callable lookup kept both the derived member and the using-imported base member when direct class lookup found a function set with the same non-object member-function signature. | fixed | `libs/circular_buffer/test//base_test_dbg` and `//space_optimized_test_dbg`; debug iterators are passed through condition calls, short-circuit RHS checks, and `assign_n(..., cb_details::make_assign_range(...))` as class temporaries. | Three temporary-lifetime holes combined in the debug iterator paths. Simple branch conditions did not lower inside a full-expression cleanup scope, so class temporaries used by condition calls could survive into the selected branch. The short-circuit RHS path checked for cleanups before emitting the RHS expression, so RHS temporaries were not destroyed before the true/false edge. Finally, output support for prvalue class temporaries bound to reference parameters only required a destructor when the class type already had layout; Boost's template `assign_range<iterator, allocator>` was known structurally but not yet layout-complete during collection, so LowIR could materialize the hidden reference argument without an `assign_range` destructor symbol or cleanup call. The fix reuses existing cleanup scopes and completes only the structured class type for non-lvalue, non-reference arguments bound to reference parameters; it adds no source reparsing or broad lifetime tracker. | `pa16:300`; `pa18:300` for the template-shaped reference-bound reducer | `pa16/tests/general/300-condition-call-argument-temporary-cleanup.t`; `pa16/tests/general/300-short-circuit-condition-rhs-temp-cleanup.t`; `pa18/tests/general/300-reference-prvalue-template-member-temp-cleanup.t` | Focused no-STL reducers returned live leaked temporaries, and the reference-bound template reducer exits `2` when the old layout-complete predicate is restored. Direct Boost-shaped `circular_base_dbg_assign_iterator_probe.cpp` segfaulted with exit `139`; generated LowIR constructed the `assign_range` hidden argument but did not emit its destructor, leaving stale debug iterators registered. | `make -C dev cppgm++ -j4`; focused PA16/PA18 regressions pass and their native executables exit `0`; placement audits for PA16 and PA18 have no new early-owner findings; the reference-bound reducer fails before and passes after the final structured-completion fix; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa16' ... make test-report-nobuild` passes `144/144`; `CPPGM_LOWIR_DIRECT_TEXT_COMPARE=1 ACTIVE_TEST_REPORT_PAS='pa18' ... make test-report-nobuild` passes `171/171`; strict direct-LowIR compare passes; focused Boost `base_test_dbg` and `space_optimized_test_dbg` pass; full `JOBS=12 ./run-cppgm-b2.sh -a libs/circular_buffer/test` passes and updates 24 targets; perf check against `/tmp/cppgm-perf-baseline-boost-frontier-current-local-gate-20260525.json` passes (`+0.51%` instructions, RSS `+2.70%`, footprint `+1.35%`). | this commit |
| fixed | `libs/circular_buffer/test//base_test` and `//space_optimized_test`; `boost::circular_buffer::at(0)` threw `boost::wrapexcept<std::out_of_range>` immediately after one `push_back`. | Direct-branch load elision rewrote a one-use load from a promoted parameter slot into a later compare source. The fix keeps the existing elision fast path but declines it only when a promoted parameter-slot load would cross a caller-saved clobber before the branch, causing the loaded value to materialize in the normal temp interval and use a callee-saved register or frame slot. | fixed | `libs/bloom/test//test_bulk_operations`; Boost `detail::umul128` declares `__uint128_t r = (__uint128_t)x * y;` inside a lazily materialized header function body. | The parser's GNU int128 extension recognized `__int128` but not hosted typedef spellings `__int128_t` and `__uint128_t` as declaration specifiers or cast/type-id specifiers. | fixed | `libs/bimap/test//xpressive`; Boost.Xpressive `static_xpression::get_width()` calls `this->get_width_(mpl_::size_t<width>())`, where `width` comes through an inherited base and the selected overload is a member function template with its own non-type parameter named `Width`. | Member-template signature collection parsed `mpl_::size_t<Width>` with class and inherited member lookup still active, so the function-template parameter name could resolve to the inherited class value instead of the member-template parameter.
